May 19, 2026 07:26 AM GMT

# Investor Presentation | Japan

# Electronic Components: Renewed Focus on Value-Added N. American Smartphone Components Plus AI Computing

In addition to proliferation of devices leveraging AI computing, we expect F3/27 earnings for suppliers of high value-added components in N. American smartphones to top consensus.

# Key Takeaways

■ OW on TDK: Top Pick. Earnings expansion for rechargeable batteries and HDD-related products.   
OW on Murata Mfg: Growing demand and utilization rates for high-value-added MLCCs.   
■ OW on Alps Alpine: High-performance camera actuators to ramp up from Apr-Jun.   
■ OW on Niterra: Continued earnings growth for replacement plugs and SPE electrostatic chucks.   
■ OW on Hirose Electric: Earnings expansion for general industrial machinery/AI server connectors.

# UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

■ We expect ABF packages for AI servers sales to increase and earnings growth to continue.   
■ We see Ibiden sustaining a competitive advantage in ABF package technology and keeping dominant market share in GPU ABF packages for AI servers.   
■ We expect earnings growth to continue due to customer and product mix improvement, even though costs increase due mainly to new lines being activated in F3/26.   
Glass cloth shortages seem likely to continue until 3Q F3/27 and F3/27 earnings to fall short of the market's overly high expectations.   
■ We see a high risk that market consensus will come down and the stock price will fall.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ We expect the firm to maintain competitiveness in automotive plugs and exhaust gas sensors, and market share to keep gradually expanding.   
Although demand for plugs is unlikely to grow substantially (Niterra boasts the top global share at 47%), we expect Niterra's market share to increase gradually and the firm to book large profits on aftermarket products.   
In automotive sensors, we expect Niterra to gradually increase its 44% global market share, and earnings to keep growing until F3/28 alongside the tightening of environmental regulations.   
In non-auto business, we believe earnings from electrostatic chucks for SPE will continue growing having bottomed in F3/24

# BULL CASE

Derived from DCF

Automotive plug & exhaust gas sensor sales exceed the base case by 4% and earnings continue to grow further. This scenario assumes OP of ¥150.6bn in F3/26, ¥179.3bn in F3/27, ¥196.5bn in F3/28, ¥225.6bn in F3/29, and uses the same FX & DCF assumptions as the base case. Bull case fair value equates to P/E of 16.7x on F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥659.4 and 15.2x on F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥721.6.

# ¥11,000

# BASE CASE

Derived from DCF

Expect profit to continue to reach new heights, driven by increased sales of automobile plugs and SPE electro static chuck: We forecast OP of ¥139.1bn in F3/26, ¥155.6bn in F3/27, ¥171.7bn in F3/28, ¥196.6bn in F3/29, assuming FX rates of ¥150/US\$, ¥170/€. Our PT is calculated from our DCF model, the conditions for which are outlined in our valuation methodology. Our base case PT equates to P/E of 15.4x on F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥592.4 and 13.7x on F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥655.8.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

■ Earnings should continue to recover having bottomed in F3/24, but this is mostly priced into the stock.   
■ We expect earnings growth of Minebea's more-than-60% share of the global market for miniature ball bearings to continue in mid/long term.   
■ We expect it will be difficult for the semiconductor & electronics segment to post a new record high in the near term.   
■ Access Solution earnings should improve thanks to cost cuts and market share gains.

# BULL CASE

# Derived from DCF

Semiconductor & Electronics and Precision Technology sales higher than in our base case: OP ¥112.2bn in F3/26, ¥129.7bn in F3/27, ¥142.5bn in F3/28, ¥154.9bn in F3/29. Same FX and DCF model assumptions as the base case. Fair value for the shares equates to 18.1x F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥237.7, 16.5x F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥261.2.

# ¥4,300

# BASE CASE

# Derived from DCF

Earnings continue to grow, driven by rising sales of core products, as the market

expects: OP ¥102.1bn in F3/26, ¥116.9bn in F3/27, ¥127.5bn in F3/28, ¥134.0bn in F3/29. FX assumption of ¥150/\$, ¥170/€. DCF model assumptions: 2.2% risk-free rate, 1.21 equity beta, 3.7% risk premium, yielding 5.2% WACC. Base case fair value equates to 15.4x F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥214.0, 14.1x F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥233.7.

# ¥3,300

# BEAR CASE

# F3/27e BPS ¥2,078 x P/B 1.0

Earnings especially in Semiconductor & Electronics and Motor, Lighting & Sensing

will be weaker than base case: OP ¥89.6bn in F3/26, ¥81.5bn in F3/27, ¥85.5bn in F3/28, ¥92.1bn in F3/29. 7.1% ROE in F3/27 is higher than cost of capital of 6.7%, but earnings are lower than market consensus and the share price declines to 1.0 x P/B.

# Risk Reward - Mabuchi Motor (6592.T)

Gradual profit growth on auto output recovery, headcount increases

# PRICE TARGET ¥1,750

Our PT is calculated from our DCF model derived from our base case, and assumes a risk-free rate of 2.2%, equity beta of 0.81, and risk premium of 3.7%, yielding WACC of 5.2%. We assume a 0% perpetual growth rate for F12/36 onwards.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

■ ROE improves gradually due to moderate recovery in profit, driven by global auto production recovery and headcount increases.   
■ Higher automotive motor volumes drive earnings growth over the longer term.

Consensus Rating Distribution

# Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

# BULL CASE

# Derived from DCF

Automotive motor output exceeds our base case by 8% thanks to demand growth and market share gains: This scenario assumes OP of ¥33.2bn in F12/26, ¥35.7bn in F12/27 and ¥38.5bn in F12/28 using the same FX and DCF assumptions as the base case. Our bull case fair value estimate equates to P/E of 18.5x (13.0x excluding net financial assets) on F12/26e diluted EPS of ¥113.5.

# ¥2,100

# BASE CASE

# Derived from DCF

Earnings recover gradually due mainly to global auto production recovery: We forecast OP of ¥27.2bn in F12/26, ¥29.3bn in F12/27 and ¥31.6bn in F12/28, assuming an FX rate of ¥150/\$. Our PT is derived from our DCF model, the conditions for which are outlined in our Valuation Methodology. Our PT equates to P/E of 19.3x (12.4x excluding net financial assets) on F12/26e diluted EPS of ¥90.7 and 17.2x on F12/27e diluted EPS of ¥101.5.

# ¥1,750

# BEAR CASE

# End-F12/26e diluted BPS ¥1,369 x P/B 0.8

Profit drops in F12/26 due to rising costs and automobile production decline: This scenario is for OP of ¥21.5bn in F12/26, ¥214bn in F12/27 and ¥19.8bn in F12/28, assuming ROE is expected to decline to 5.6% in FY12/26, remaining above the cost of capital of 5.2%; however, the market consensus has been revised down sharply, and the share price is expected to fall to a P/B of 0.8x, but end-F12/25 net financial assets/share of ¥645 and shareholder returns underpin the share price.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ We expect Passive Components and Sensor Application earnings to improve from F3/26 after the bottom in F3/25.   
■ We envision only modest LiB profit in F3/27, but sustained LiB profit growth in the mid-long term as value-added silicon cathode sales expand.   
■ HDD related business earnings recovery to continue after turning to the black in F3/25.

# Risk Reward Themes

New Data Era: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

# BULL CASE

# Derived from DCF

Earnings from rechargeable batteries, passive components, sensors, and HDD heads ahead of base case. OP ¥294.3bn in

F3/26, ¥350.9bn in F3/27, ¥409.0bn in F3/28. Same currency and DCF model assumptions as base case. Fair value equates to P/E 29.0x on F3/27e diluted EPS ¥138.0, P/E 24.8x on F3/28e diluted EPS ¥161.0.

# ¥4,000

# BASE CASE

# Derived from DCF

Only moderate LIB profit growth in F3/27, but in mid-long term, profit expansion not only for LIB, but also for HDDs, sensors and passive components. OP ¥267.9bn in F3/26, ¥309.8bn in F3/27, ¥348.1bn in F3/28. Assumes ¥150/USD, ¥170/EUR; DCF model assumptions: risk-free rate 2.2%, equity beta 1.27, risk premium 3.7%, yielding WACC 6.3%. Zero growth from F3/36. Fair value equates to P/E 26.2x F3/27e diluted EPS ¥122.1, 23.3x F3/28 diluted EPS ¥137.2.

# ¥3,200

# BEAR CASE

# End-F3/27 diluted BPS ¥1,091 x P/B 1.0x

Limited profit growth due to rechargeable battery slowdown. OP ¥168.2bn in F3/26, ¥186.6bn in F3/27, ¥215.5bn in F3/27. Same currency assumptions as base case. ROE 6.4% in F3/26 and 7.0% in F3/27, almost same as the cost of capital of 6.9%, and P/B equates to 1.0x.

Note: e = MS estimates; Stock price as of the May 15 close for Risk Reward Chart  
Source: Company data, MS

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

■ We expect a significant expansion in profits from smartphone camera actuators and components starting in F3/27.   
■ We expect only limited improvement in performance for the Module & System and Mobility segments.   
We expect continued earnings expansion that falls short of the mid-term plan's final-year (F3/28) OP target of ¥71.0bn, but significantly exceeds market consensus.

Consensus Rating Distribution   
![](images/3204b0429d12494cf54ad99268aba030ad81a596a29360975f88e460acdf30fa.jpg)

# Risk Reward Themes

Contrarian: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

# BULL CASE

# Derived from DCF

Earnings improve at a pace exceeding the mid-term management plan. OP ¥41.9bn in F3/26, ¥69.3bn in F3/27, ¥91.6bn in F3/28, ¥101.3bn in F3/29. Same currency and DCF model assumptions as base case. Fair value equates to P/E 16.6x F3/27 diluted EPS ¥240.6, and 12.5x F3/28 diluted EPS ¥320.3, P/B 1.8x end-F3/27e diluted BPS of ¥2,273.

# ¥4,000

# BASE CASE

# Derived from DCF

Although below the mid-term plan, we expect continued strong profit growth driven by camera actuators. We forecast OP ¥37.3bn in F3/26, ¥50.8bn in F3/27, ¥68.1bn in F3/28, ¥77.8bn in F3/29 Base case assumes ¥150/USD. Fair value equates to P/E 15.7 x F3/27e diluted EPS ¥184.5 and 11.6x F3/28e EPS ¥250.3.

# ¥2,900

# BEAR CASE

# F3/27e diluted BPS ¥1,995 x P/B 07x

Sluggish output of high-end smartphones and declining market share cause smartphone camera actuator sales to fall 10% short of our base case. OP ¥314bn in F3/26, ¥25.7bn in F3/27, ¥25.7bn in F3/28, ¥27.7bn in F3/29. ROE 4.5% in F3/27, lower than the 5.5% cost of capital we assume, and the share drops to 0.7x of F3/27e diluted BPS.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

We forecast an increase in use of photolithography products in industrial machinery and mobility markets. The firm has high global market share for auto products, for which it is increasing personnel for liquid crystal devices. There is also growth potential in optical transceivers and AI server receivers. However, competition is intensifying in the profitable high-end smartphone field, including for MEMS silicon devices.

# Risk Reward – Meiko Electronics (6787.T)

Rapid Earnings Growth Driven by Aggressive Investment Largely Priced in

# PRICE TARGET ¥29,200

Our price target is ¥29,200, based on a DCF valuation (risk-free rate 2.2%; beta 1.5; risk premium 3.7%; WACC 4.9%). Beyond our 3-year base earnings models, we also build forecasts for F3/29–30 into our DCF-based valuation. Our assumption from F3/31 onward is that the firm maintains FCF/share of ¥2,300.
Our price target equates to 30.0x our F3/27e EPS of ¥974.1. Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

Our scenario has the firm benefitting from production capacity enhancements undertaken to meet growing demand. We evaluate positively its proactive stance on expanding production capacity for build-up substrates, including those for satellite communications and high-end smartphones. In our view, Meiko's expertise in producing build-up substrates in Vietnam will enable it to benefit from growing demand for high layer/density buildup PCBs, as well as from efforts to move supply chains out of Greater China.

# Risk Reward – Hirose Electric (6806.T)

Sustained high profitability and strong sales growth

# PRICE TARGET ¥26,300

Our price target is ¥26,300, based on a DCF valuation (risk-free rate 2.2%; beta 0.9; risk premium 3.7%; WACC 5.2%). Beyond our three-year forecasts, we also build forecasts for F3/29–30 into our DCF-based valuation. Our assumptions for F3/31 onward are that the firm maintains FCF/share of ¥1,450. Our price target equates to 24.0x our F3/27e EPS of ¥1,096.5. Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$.

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

The equity story is the continued top-line growth across applications through the acquisition of high-margin projects. We like the continued high level of sales growth relative to historical levels, while maintaining a high-profitability structure enabled by its semi-fabless production model. We believe that if the equity market comes to view profit growth as a mid-term trend, there is further upside to valuations.

# Risk Reward – Japan Aviation Electronics Industry (6807.T)

Key to profitability recovery is regaining North American smartphone share

# PRICE TARGET ¥2,600

Our price target is ¥2,600, based on a DCF valuation (risk-free rate 2.2%; beta 1.0; risk premium 3.7%; WACC 4.8%). Beyond our three-year forecasts, we also build forecasts for F3/29–30 into our DCF-based valuation. Our assumptions for F3/31 onward are that the firm maintains FCF/share of ¥130. Our price target equates to 24.0x our F3/27e EPS of ¥108.3. Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$.

Consensus Price Target Distribution

Source: Refinitiv, MS

![](images/536802c27f4868ba77a59c748a375b1c5c9246c674e44a8b58168e83674c09b3.jpg)

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

The equity story centers on margin improvement driven by a recovery in North American smartphone connector share. Top-line growth is expected to continue, driven not only by increased sales of wire harnesses for ADAS cameras but also by connectors for BEV chargers and expanded connector sales in India. However, for the stock to rise, we believe profitability must improve through a rebound in North American B-to-B connector market share and expansion of the relatively high-margin aerospace business.

# Risk Reward - CMK (6958.T)

Further vehicle electronification and growth in new business areas awaited

# PRICE TARGET ¥640

-6.3 Our price target is ¥640, based on a DCF valuation (risk-free rate 2.2%; beta 1.3; risk premium 3.7%; WACC 5.1%). Beyond our 3-year base earnings models, we also build forecasts for F3/29–30 into our DCF-based valuation, and our assumptions for F3/31 onward are that the firm maintains FCF/share of ¥55. Our price target equates to 28.5x our F3/27e EPS of ¥22.4.
-0.2 Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

Our scenario calls for earnings growth driven by automotive PCBs. We maintain the view that earnings will resume growing after bottoming in F3/26 1H, driven by recovery in production mainly at J-OEM automakers. However, we assign an EW rating as the ramp-up in demand for Japanese Tier-1 suppliers' integrated ECUs appears to be delayed, and the negative impact from sluggish car demand in Europe due to the economic slowdown is likely to persist. That said, the high quality and reliability of CMK's buildup PCBs honed across many years, and its supply system which allows the same boards to be produced globally (including in new facilities in Thailand), continue to be well regarded.

# BULL CASE

Improves productivity to meet rising demand

OP and EPS forecasts of ¥2.8bn/¥33.8 in F3/26, ¥3.5bn/¥28.1 in F3/27, ¥5.3bn/¥46.3 in F3/28, ¥7.7bn/¥68.7 in F3/29, and ¥10.5bn/¥95.4 in F3/30. Our calculations also assume that from F3/31 onward the firm can maintain FCF/share at ¥95. The fair value equates to 4.38x our F3/27E EPS of ¥28.1 and 1.1x BPS of ¥1,092.3.

# ¥1,230

# BASE CASE

BU sales for Integretad ECU in earnest from F3/27

Our calculations also assume that from F3/31 onward the firm can maintain FCF/share at ¥55. Our PT equates to 28.5x our F3/27E EPS of ¥22.4 and 0.6x BPS of ¥1,086.7.

# BEAR CASE

Weak automotive production, slow uptake for ADAS

OP and EPS forecasts of ¥2.8bn/¥33.8 in F3/26, ¥1.6bn/¥9.8 in F3/27, ¥2.1bn/¥15.4 in F3/28, ¥2.4bn/¥18.2 in F3/29, and ¥2.8bn/¥22.4 in F3/30. Our calculations also assume that from F3/31 onward the firm can maintain FCF/share at ¥25. The fair value equates to 22.4x our F3/27E EPS of ¥9.8 and 0.2x BPS of ¥1,074.1.

# Risk Reward – Daishinku (6962.T)

Demand for new devices accelerating and production system upgrades awaited

# PRICE TARGET ¥610

Our price target is ¥610, based on a DCF valuation (risk-free rate 2.2%; beta 1.2; risk premium 3.7%; WACC 4.8%). Beyond our 3-year base earnings models, we also build forecasts for F3/29–30 into our DCF-based valuation, and our assumptions for F3/31 onward are that the firm maintains FCF/share of ¥70. Our price target equates to 32.3x our F3/27e EPS of ¥18.9. Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

Sales weighting for photolithographic products has risen as demand for high-frequency products from clients has increased. We give the firm high marks for progressively ramping up capacity of TCXO, demand for which has been rising for use in communication modules or GPS applications. However, we believe Daishinku still needs time to build a network to expand sales of the Arkh. Series, which has technological advantages in terms of both cost and productivity. We also think the firm will continue facing downward pressure on prices, esp. for consumer applications. Ongoing production issues could also become a bottleneck in the demand upcycle, and countermeasures should be closely monitored.

# BULL CASE

# Telecom/auto growth speeds up

OP and EPS forecasts of ¥0.9bn/¥7.1 in F3/26, ¥1.8bn/¥31.4 in F3/27, ¥2.8bn/¥53.5 in F3/28, ¥4.2bn/¥81.8 in F3/29, and ¥5.3bn/¥110.0 in F3/30. Our calculations also assume that from F3/31 onward the firm can maintain FCF/share at ¥110. The fair value equates to 38.8x our F3/27E EPS of ¥31.4 and 1.1x BPS of ¥1,124.8.

# UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

■ A decline in profits in F9/26 appears unavoidable, and even if earnings begin to recover from F9/27, it is likely to take some time before a full-fledged recovery materializes.

■ With inventory levels high, it will take time for utilization rates to rise and profit to recover fully.

■ We see major potential for applied optical technologies.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

\- Needs to further strengthen core components business and restructure solution business.
- Sale of KDDI shares and improve shareholder return should improve ROE. However, it is not clear which businesses will be the earnings drivers and whether or not earnings improvement will continue in the mid/longer term.
- We expect ROE to improve to $4.6\%$ in F3/29 from $0.7\%$ in F3/25, which is still lower than cost of capital of $5.4\%$ and management's target of $7.0\%$ .

Source: Refinitiv, MS

# BULL CASE

# Derived from DCF

Sales top the base case by 5% as parts demand recovers: OP ¥131.4bn in F3/26, ¥160.5bn in F3/27, ¥197.0bn in F3/28, ¥225.9bn in F3/29. Same FX & DCF model assumptions as the base case. Bull case fair value equates to P/E of 35.4x on F3/27 diluted EPS of ¥98.9.

# ¥3,500

# BASE CASE

# Derived from DCF

Expect BS structure change by sales of KDDI shares and improvement of return to shareholders. However, it is not clear which business will be earnings drivers. OP ¥102.4bn in F3/26, ¥121.5bn in F3/27, ¥138.0bn in F3/28, ¥166.9bn in F3/29. FX assumptions: ¥150/US\$, ¥170/€. Base case fair value equates to 39.4 x F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥71.1, 35.7x F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥78.4.

# ¥2,800

# BEAR CASE

# F3/27e diluted BPS ¥2,050 x P/B 0.65

Limited improvement in components business earnings. Improvement of capital efficiency limited, although shareholder return improvement continues. OP ¥77.4bn in F3/26, OP ¥96.5bn in F3/27, OP ¥103.0bn in F3/28, OP ¥118.5bn in F3/29. Same FX assumptions as the base case. ROE of 2.5% in F3/27 and 2.5% in F3/28 remains below capital costs of 5.4%, prompting the stock to trade on P/B of 0.65x.

Note: e = MS estimates; Stock price as of the May 15 close for Risk Reward Chart  
Source: Company data, MS

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

■ Earnings continue to recover, but surpassing the F3/22 record high will likely take considerable time.   
■ We expect continued earnings recovery in the core MLCC business..   
■ We expect the firm to maintain its tech competitiveness in mainstay MLCCs.   
■ We expect sales growth for power inductors used in 5G smartphones and base stations to continue.

Consensus Rating Distribution

# Derived from DCF

MLCC sales exceed the base case. OP ¥25.1bn in F3/26, ¥44.0bn in F3/27, ¥56.1bn in F3/28. Same FX & DCF model assumptions as the base case. Fair value equates to P/E 26.2x on F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥248.0 and 20.7x on F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥314.3.

# Derived from DCF

While higher MLCC utilization is driving earnings improvement, a full recovery will take time: OP ¥22.5bn in F3/26, ¥31.8bn in F3/27, ¥39.1bn in F3/28. FX assumption: ¥150/US\$. Base case fair value equates to P/E 25.9x on F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥181.3, and 21.3x on F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥221.1.

# F3/27e diluted BPS ¥2,403 x P/B 0.83

Stays competitive in core products, but slowing end demand drags profits down. OP ¥18.4bn in F3/26, OP ¥23.0bn in F3/27, ¥25.0bn in F3/28. Same FX and DCF assumptions as in the base case. ROE 4.4% in F3/26, 5.8% in F3/27, below capital costs of 6.7% and with earnings lower than market expectation, P/B falls to 0.83x.

Note: e = MS estimates; Stock price as of the May 15 close for Risk Reward Chart

# OVERWEIGHT THESIS

In F3/26, we expect MLCC sales to increase and LiB losses to shrink.   
■ Utilization rates in the MLCC space are likely to remain high as demand for use in AI servers continues to rise, and we thus expect currently high profits to expand further.   
■ We expect Murata to remain competitive in MLCCs and other products over the long term.   
In F3/27 onward, we expect module earnings to improve as sales of RF modules rise.

# Risk Reward Themes

New Data Era: Positive Secular Growth: Positive

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

# BULL CASE

Derived from DCF

Demand for end products such as smartphones and autos is stronger than in the base case. OP assumptions: ¥318.5bn in F3/26, ¥430.2bn in F3/27, ¥526.7bn in F3/28. Same FX & DCF model assumptions as the base case. Bull case fair value equates to P/E of 34.3x on F3/27 diluted EPS of ¥175.1 and 28.1x on F3/28 diluted EPS of ¥213.3.

# ¥6,000

# BASE CASE

Derived from DCF

Earnings continue to grow and beat consensus over the medium/long-term due to technological advantages & competitiveness, especially in MLCCs.

Assumptions: OP ¥270.6bn in F3/26, ¥372.8bn in F3/27, ¥459.3bn in F3/28; FX ¥150/US\$, ¥170/€; DCF model (same as valuation methodology): 2.2% risk-free rate, 1.09 equity beta, 3.7% risk premium, yielding 6.2% WACC; zero growth from F3/36. Base case fair value equates to P/E 31.9x on F3/27e diluted EPS of ¥160.0 and 26.0x on F3/28e diluted EPS of ¥195.9.

# ¥5,100

# BEAR CASE

# ¥2,200

F3/27e BPS ¥1,448x P/B 1.5

Murata remains competitive, but final demand for its components is lower than we envision. OP assumptions: ¥240.7bn in F3/26, ¥279.1bn in F3/27, ¥310.2bn in F3/28. Same FX assumptions as the base case. Assumes ROE moves from 9.1% in F3/25 to 7.5% in F3/26 and 8.2% in F3/27, higher than our capital cost estimate of 6.2%, but with earnings missing market expectations, P/B drops to 1.5x.

# Risk Reward – Nichicon (6996.T)

AEC earnings to recover gradually

# PRICE TARGET ¥2,100

Our price target is ¥2,100, based on a DCF valuation (risk-free rate 2.2%; beta 1.0; risk premium 3.7%; WACC 4.7%). Beyond our 3-year base earnings models, we also build forecasts for F3/29–30 into our DCF-based valuation, and our assumptions for F3/31 onward are that the firm maintains FCF/share of ¥105. Our price target equates to 22.7x our F3/27E EPS of ¥92.3. Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$.

# EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

The equity story is centered on increased sales of high value-added, high-capacity capacitors and the stable growth of the NECST business. From F3/27, we expect the capacitor business to expand, driven primarily by large-size products for AI servers and film capacitors for xEV applications, while competition in general-purpose products is likely to remain intense amid rivalry with Chinese players. We expect NECST business to deliver stable growth, supported by the wider adoption of residential energy storage systems.

# Risk Reward – Nippon Chemi-Con (6997.T)

Earnings to recover gradually, but be careful about risk of share dilution

# PRICE TARGET ¥880

Our price target is ¥880, based on a DCF valuation (risk-free rate 2.2%; beta 1:1; risk premium 3.7%; WACC 4.2%). Beyond our 3-year base earnings models, we also build forecasts for F3/29–30 into our DCF-based valuation, and our assumptions for F3/31 onward are that the firm maintains FCF/share of ¥130. Our price target equates to 8.6x our F3/27E EPS of ¥102.3. Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$.

# UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

The equity story centers on earnings growth from higher sales of aluminum electrolytic capacitors for industrial and xEV applications. Although demand for high value-added capacitors for AI servers and xEVs is driving an earnings upturn, we think earnings are unlikely to return to the F3/23 peak in the near term, and profitability improvement will remain limited amid stronger overseas and MLCC competition. There is also a risk that Class B shares may be converted on favorable terms, or that Class A shares may need to be redeemed in cash with a premium added to the ¥10bn investment amount, as OP fell short of ¥8.8bn in F3/25.

# Risk Reward - KOA (6999.T)

Medium-term plan earnings targets set a high bar

# PRICE TARGET ¥1,540

Our DCF-based price target is ¥1,540 (risk-free rate 2.2%, beta 1.2, risk premium 3.7%, WACC 5.0%). In addition to our three-year forecasts, we reference our forecasts for F3/29-30. For F3/31 onward, we assume FCF per share of ¥120. Our PT is equivalent to 17.3x our F3/27 EPS estimate of ¥88.9. Our forex rate assumption for the next quarter and thereafter is ¥150/\$

# UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

The equity story is that the negative impact of higher fixed costs associated with building up production capacity is set to continue. In the automotive resistor market, we think the rise of competing Taiwanese manufacturers is being watched. We expect KOA to enter an earnings recovery phase from F3/26, but given the impact of increased fixed costs from capacity expansion, we think the current medium-term management plan's earnings targets will be a stretch.

# Risk Reward Themes

Pricing Power: Negative

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

# BULL CASE

Achieves medium-term earnings targets

OP and EPS forecasts of ¥3.7bn/¥86.2 in F3/26, ¥6.0bn/¥102.3 in F3/27, ¥8.2bn/¥142.7 in F3/28, ¥10.5bn/¥188.5 in F3/29, and ¥12.4bn/¥223.5 in F3/30. Our calculations also assume that from F3/31 onward the firm can maintain FCF/share at ¥225. The fair value equates to 31.0x our F3/27E EPS of ¥102.3 and 1.5x BPS of ¥2,180.4.

Earnings remain sluggish as sales growth slows

Our calculations also assume that from F3/31 onward the firm can maintain FCF/share at ¥70. The fair value equates to 9.1x our F3/27E EPS of ¥67.3 and 0.3x BPS of ¥2,145.4.

# Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in MS is 12 to 18 months.

# Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
